  [XX] turn 0  Q: What is the relationship between Seth's age and Brooke's age?
             gold='Seth is twice as old as Brooke.'  pred=''  gen='Let me think. So, the problem says that Seth is twice as old as Brooke. And in 2 years, the sum of their ages will be 28. We need to find the relationship between their ages. Wait, but the first sentence already says Seth is twice as old as Brooke. So maybe the question is asking for the current ages or something else? Let me check again.'
  [OK] turn 1  Q: What will be the sum of their ages in 2 years?
             gold='28'  pred='28'  gen="Let's find the sum of their ages in 2 years. We know that Seth is twice as old as Brooke. Let's denote Brooke's current age as $ B $, so Seth's current age is $ 2B $. In 2 years, Brooke will be $ B + 2 $ years old and Seth will be $ 2B + 2 $ years old. The sum of their ages in 2 years is $ (B + 2) + (2B + 2) = 3B + 4 $. According to the problem, this sum is 28."
  [XX] turn 2  Q: What is the equation representing the sum of their ages in 2 years?
             gold='3x + 4 = 28'  pred=''  gen="Let's denote Brooke's current age as $ B $. Since Seth is twice as old as Brooke, Seth's current age is $ 2B $. In 2 years, Brooke's age will be $ B + 2 $ and Seth's age will be $ 2B + 2 $. The sum of their ages in 2 years is given as 28. Therefore, the equation is:"
  [XX] turn 3  Q: What is the value of x after solving the equation?
             gold='8'  pred=None  gen="Let's solve the equation step by step."
  [OK] turn 4  Q: How old is Seth?
             gold='16'  pred='16'  gen="We are given that Brooke is 8 years old and Seth is twice as old as Brooke. Therefore, Seth's age is $ 2 \\times 8 = 16 $ years."

  [OK] turn 0  Q: How many additional meters does Tyson need to run each day according to his coach's advice?
             gold='1000'  pred='1000'  gen='The coach wants Tyson to run 1/5 times more meters than he currently does. This means he needs to run his current distance plus 1/5 of that distance. So, the total distance he should run is 5000 + (1/5 * 5000) = 5000 + 1000 = 6000 meters. Therefore, the additional distance he needs to run each day is 6000 - 5000 = 6000 - 5000 = 1000 meters.'

  [OK] turn 1  Q: How much is Juice Box C?

  [OK] turn 2  Q: How much more is Juice Box C than Juice Box B?
